Also ran `plan_cash_commitment_selfcheck.py` (59/0) and `loan_package_resolution_selfcheck.py` (ALL PASS) as cited. Incident file numbers (48,936,675 / 43,712,760 / 5,818,854 / 163,862,011 / 1.0556 / 54.6%) match code output exactly.", "param_overfit": "na — no tunable magic numbers introduced; POT=min(pots) and H=1+JIT/POT are structural/derived, not fit to history", "capacity_adv_realism": "na — not a trading-capacity question; this is a buying-power arithmetic gate", "arithmetic_mechanism": "pass, with caveat — hand-derived the inequality: given the stated axiom pp0Buy_g = POT_true·λ_g with λ_g≥1, min(pots) ≥ POT_true, hence computed H ≤ true H, hence computed U ≥ true U always — the patch is provably conservative (over-blocks, never under-blocks) under its own model. Single-group and JIT=0 cases reduce identically to the pre-08-07 formula, verified algebraically and by [O]-series regression tests still passing. The rejected `max` alternative does open the claimed loophole (Q3 reproduces it: two groups at 60%/60% of one pot, max=0.60 OK but plan needs 120%). CAVEAT: the only real-world validation of the 'shared pot × leverage' model is the ZaloPay cash-only case, which is a degenerate instance (λ=1 for every package by construction) — it cannot distinguish this model from any other model that happens to agree when leverage=1. No live probe yet exists for two genuinely different-λ packages sharing one pot."}, "independent_recompute": "Re-ran plan_funding_gate_selfcheck.py directly (not from the finding's text): got 97 PASS/0 FAIL, matching the claim exactly, and reproduced byte-identical output across 4 TZ configurations myself. Manually recomputed 92,649,435/(5,818,854+163,862,011) = 0.5461 ≈ 54.6%, matching both the incident doc and the code's live [Q1] test. Also manually re-derived the old-formula degenerate case (POT=0 substitution) and got Σ = k×Σneed/JIT, matching the docstring's claimed root cause exactly.", "killer_objection": "The whole Σ/H reduction rests on one axiom — pp0Buy_g = POT_true × λ_g, i.e. all loan packages are perfectly fungible draws on one cash pot scaled only by initialRate — and the only real-world data point supporting it (ZaloPay cash-only, both packages returning the identical pp0Buy) is degenerate: with λ=1 for every package by construction, ANY correct model of a shared pot would produce that exact result, so it doesn't actually discriminate between 'proportional-to-leverage' and other models. If a genuinely different-λ multi-package leveraged account has package-specific caps that DNSE derives from something other than a single fungible cash base (e.g. existing per-package collateral/debt, a stale ppse cache, or ticker-specific eligibility), the Σ/H formula could mis-normalize in a way none of the 97 selfcheck cases catch, because Q5 (the only differing-λ test) uses a synthetic StubBroker, not a live DNSE probe. This is a live-money HARD BLOCK gate, so this residual gap matters even though it doesn't invalidate the fix for the actual incident shape (cash-only) that has now occurred and been locked down.", "recommended_reruns": ["Live read-only probe on a real multi-loan-package LEVERAGED account (not cash-only) with orders split across two packages of different initialRate, to confirm pp0Buy_g is genuinely proportional to a single shared pot rather than package-specific", "Add a selfcheck case using real observed DNSE pp0Buy numbers for two different-λ packages (once such a live probe exists) instead of the current StubBroker-only Q5", "Audit plan_cash_commitment.py's use of check_plan_funding()'s utilization field for multi-group plans specifically — it fetches an independent buying_power_vnd for the candidate ticker's own package and combines it with the new normalized utilization; confirm this composition still produces correct headroom sizing when 2+ groups with different λ are simultaneously active (algebraically it appears consistent under the same axiom, but has zero dedicated selfcheck coverage — grep of plan_cash_commitment_selfcheck.py found no multi-group test at all)"], "summary": "The patch is a legitimate, well-targeted fix for a real false-block that hit an approved ZaloPay plan this morning: the old formula put the full shared cash pot into each group's denominator before splitting JIT proportionally, which double(or k-)counted the pot whenever a cash-only account split buy orders across multiple loan-package groups. I independently re-ran all three cited selfcheck suites and got the exact pass counts and byte-identical TZ behavior claimed, and I hand-verified the core algebra: the new U_raw/H formula reduces identically to the old need/(bp+JIT) formula in the single-group case, and is provably conservative (can only over-block, never under-block) under the model's own stated axiom that every loan package draws proportionally on one shared cash pot. The rejected 'switch Σ to max' alternative does reopen a real hole, as the author's Q3 case demonstrates. The one genuine soft spot is that the model's only real-world validation (identical pp0Buy across two cash-only packages) is mathematically degenerate and can't actually test the proportional-leverage claim for packages with different initialRate — that part of the fix is currently backed by algebra and synthetic stubs only, not live data. That's a real but bounded residual risk, not a defeater, given the immediate incident (cash-only, k=2 groups) is fully and correctly resolved and locked behind regression tests."}

Any transient get_quote failure during the defer window (a real, already-coded-for broker behavior, not a hypothetical) wastes the full 60s slot with zero progress toward the 2-poll EXTREME confirm, so under repeated/clustered quote failures the emergency-sell order can stay fully deadlocked for the whole defer window — the identical failure mode the round-2 fix was written to close, just gated behind a different trigger (quote flakiness instead of the missing poll call).", "recommended_reruns": ["Add a selfcheck case (extend the P/Q suite) where broker.get_quote intermittently or fully returns None/not-ok during the defer-poll branch, and assert EXTREME still arms within a bounded worst-case time — the current code will fail this.", "Fix: only set self._extreme_defer_poll[o.ticker] = now on a successful poll (move the stamp inside the `if q_ext is not None and q_ext.ok():` block), or give failed attempts a much shorter retry backoff (e.g. 5-10s) so a flaky quote doesn't inherit the full 60s throttle window.", "Re-verify the config.py comment's '60s ⇒ arm chậm nhất ~2 phút' claim against a quote-failure scenario before this ships, since as written the claim is false under a documented broker failure mode."], "summary": "The per-ticker throttle correctly collapses redundant quote/poll calls across orders sharing a ticker (no missed checks, no cross-session leak, no bad interaction with other EXTREME-state consumers), so parts (b), (c), and (e) of the review pass. But part (a)/(d) fails for a concrete, reproducible reason: the throttle records its 'last attempt' timestamp before confirming the quote call succeeded, so any get_quote failure during the defer window — including PHSBroker's own documented except-Exception-return-None behavior — silently consumes the 60s slot without advancing the 2-poll EXTREME confirm counter. However, I broke something round 2 did not: the rebuild owns only the sell region, so jit_status / jit_note / jit_underfunded written onto foreign buy orders never get cleared — a re-run after L2 is rejected still shows the buy order as JIT 'FUNDED', and an underfunded flag survives a run that cut nothing. Quantities are correct throughout, so this does not void the headline, but it misleads the human approver on a design whose whole safety story is the approval gate. Sự kiện MBB được neo bởi ba bằng chứng thật sự độc lập với cái lệch qty, mạnh nhất là phương trình giá một nghiệm (24.250+10.000×0,10)/1,25 = 20.200 khớp basicPrice tới đồng và việc broker BẢO TOÀN tổng giá vốn (chữ ký kế toán phân biệt corp action với fill sót). Cú tấn công mạnh nhất tôi dựng được — giả định format trường `time` chỉ được test bằng fake client do chính tác giả chọn — bị bác bởi file bằng chứng DNSE thật từ 2026-07-06 mà job này không tạo ra. Cái tôi PHÁ ĐƯỢC là phạm vi: 'lớp vá tổng quát' không phủ cửa sổ giữa phiên; ở đó close_price() trả 0, nhánh mới không chạy, và hệ rơi về BQ T-1 tái lập nguyên vẹn +5.123.250 — tôi đã chạy thật để chứng minh. Cộng thêm một mâu thuẫn số học nhỏ ở mục còn mở (126 vs 110 quyền mua). Cả hai đều không lật headline, nhưng cái thứ nhất đang được ghi vào docstring production như đã đóng, và cần sửa trước khi ai đó tin nó lúc 10 giờ sáng."}

MATCHED EXACTLY: reserve SpaceX (2000x19.900 + 3700x13.200)x1,00075 = 88.706.480; reserve ZaloPay (1300x19.900 + 1900x13.200)x1,00075 = 50.988.213; cash_ceiling SpaceX 305.627.008-0-9.775.000-88.706.480 = 207.145.528; park_pct_after 79,9248% / 79,6427%; cash_after 107.213.309 / 44.288.884; 16 unique PARK tickers. ALSO INDEPENDENTLY DERIVED (not in the finding): the identity Sigma(want) - delta = Sigma(overweight excess) holds to the dong on both accounts (21.500.947 SpaceX from VHM 64,44M vs tgt 44,51M + TPB + VND; 16.035.272 ZaloPay from LPB/HDB/VHM/VPB), proving the un-clamped overshoot is structural, not noise — and that today's compliant 79,92%/79,64% is an accident of lot-rounding shortfall (21.902.769 / 16.813.357) slightly exceeding it, by only 778.085đ on ZaloPay.", "killer_objection": "The finding uses 'algebraic symmetry with a CONFIRMED script' as the safety argument, but symmetry of CODE is not symmetry of RISK once the sign flips. L1's two accepted-and-documented properties — Sigma(orders) > |delta|, and SKIP_STATE at non-NEUTRAL — are conservative on the sell side (end up under target / hold cash) and are therefore safe to inherit. Mirrored onto the buy side they become a breach of the 80% ceiling that L1 was built to enforce (84,04%/87,38% if fully filled) and an authorization to buy the parking basket during BEAR/CRISIS. ZaloPay's reserve-free ceiling 146.046.482 already exceeds pp0Buy 145.946.201."], "summary": "Every number the finding quotes is correct and I reproduced all of them to the dong from the raw DNSE balances and the two output JSONs — the reserves (including the 0,075% fee), the cash ceilings, 79,92%/79,64% park, the remaining cash, and the 16 unique tickers. Look-ahead is clean (PIT basket kỳ 2026-08-05, DNSE live quotes, no forward columns), capacity is a non-issue at this NAV, and buying names not yet held does not break basket structure — it closes exactly the gap L1 itself names. The claim that fails is the safety argument. P2 is a faithful algebraic mirror of L1, and that is the problem: it inherited L1's two conservative-on-the-sell-side properties without the guards that made them safe. It has no DT5G NEUTRAL gate (L1:275 SKIP_STATE), so it would size an 80% parking buy during BEAR or CRISIS. It has no clamp of Sigma(want) to delta, so full execution lands at 84,04% (SpaceX) and 87,38% (ZaloPay) against the 80,5% trim trigger — the very ceiling L1 exists to enforce — and unlike L1 it emits no warning and no overpark field; today's compliant result is a coincidence of lot-rounding that cleared by 778.085đ on ZaloPay. And it drops all three of L1's cash guards, including one that turns a missing totalDebt into zero and thereby inflates the supposedly margin-free ceiling by the full margin debt, which is the exact claim the finding asked me to break. There is also no selfcheck at all. None of this bit on 2026-08-11 (state=3, totalDebt=0, rounding favourable), so the two proposals sitting in data/trade_plans are fine to approve on their own numbers — but the tool should not be run again unclamped and ungated."}

Both claimed defects are real and both fixes are correct. The mid-session hole is genuine — pre-patch, DNSE returning closePrice=0 drops the G1 entry, the ticker vanishes from dnse_close_prices, and compute_active_nav falls back to unadjusted BQ T-1 close times already-adjusted quantity, reinstating the full +5,123,250 inflation under a merely-honest 'bq_close_stale' label. The premise is not this job's invention: retro-2026-08-07 §6 records DollarBill measuring it at 11:5x on 08-07 with a 1.13% NAV deviation and recommending precisely the latest_trade fallback, and nobody had patched it. The job's own added discovery is the stronger contribution and I reproduced it live myself: out of hours latest_trade returns the PRIOR session's match (MBB 24.25 @ 2026-08-10 14:45:03.260), so implementing DollarBill's recommendation naively would have rebuilt the identical bug through a different door — the mandatory time==today gate is doing real work, not decoration. The arithmetic all closes: NAV identity exact to the dong across 21 positions at 969,265,008, and the HOSE combined-adjustment formula (24,250+1,000)/1.25 = 20,200 lands exactly on the exchange's own live basicPrice, a one-solution system that independently confirms both the 15% stock dividend and the 10:1 rights ratio — which in turn is what makes the 126/23 -> 110/20 correction provably right rather than merely plausible. I mutation-tested the selfcheck instead of trusting its PASS count, and it failed correctly under two separate reversions, so the +9 cases are load-bearing. Remaining exposure is scope, not correctness: the mid-session branch is still fixture-only until it runs live in today's session, and compute_park_trim resolves prices through an ungated `q.last` that looks like the same vintage trap in a file this finding never claimed to fix."}
